Flights In Europe Face Endless Bomb Threats: “This Is Only The Beginning”

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

It has been a rather rough week for many airlines in Europe, as dozens of flights have been diverted, canceled, or delayed, due to bomb threats. There’s obviously some sort of a coordinated effort here, but it’s hard to know what exactly is going on, beyond just the desire to create chaos (thanks to 1990_ for flagging this)…

The concept of bomb threats being called in on flights is nothing new. Sometimes people are dumb enough to call in a bomb threat because they’re running late for a flight, and think that’s the best way not to miss it. Other times people will call in bomb threats as a prank.

Rarely is a bomb threat actually called in on a flight where there’s a bomb. That comes down to two reasons — it’s exceedingly rare for flights to be bombed (thank goodness), and if someone actually wants to take down a plane, they wouldn’t issue a warning, since that kind of defeats the purpose.

So that brings us to what has gone on in Europe this week, particularly on Tuesday, Thursday, and again today (Friday). Dozens of flights across airlines have had bomb threats called in on them, and airlines generally have to take these seriously. This has now happened to over 50 flights, departing from roughly a dozen countries.

In some cases the bomb threats were called in before takeoff, so passengers could be deplaned, allowing a search to happen before departure. In other cases, the threats came in mid-flight, leading to diversions, and even fighter jet escorts, in some situations.

In no case were any credible threats found, though. It goes without saying that this is incredibly frustrating for all parties. This is a massive waste of time and resources, given that these kinds of threats are consistently fake. At the same time, governments and airlines have to take these threats seriously, because imagine what would happen if a threat were proven to be real, and it was ignored (personally I think they should just be ignored, but that’s neither here nor there).

It’s not known for sure who is behind this attack, or just how centrally coordinated it is, rather than there being a copycat element to this.

Some emails taking credit for these attacks were signed as being from “the fighters of the Male State,” referencing a Russian far right, ultra nationalist movement that was founded in 2016. However, in a Telegram post today, the group’s founder denied involvement in this, and said the group hasn’t even existed since 2021.

Another email sent to media outlets regarding these attacks said that “this is only the beginning,” and threatened further attacks (or at least threats of attacks) across European transport.

The timing of this is likely not a coincidence, as Ukrainian president Volodymyr Zelensky is currently visiting Europe for a series of meetings regarding the war with Russia.

It goes without saying that this could get messy. If this is really coming out of Russia, it’ll be hard for European authorities to hold them accountable. And it’s also easy enough for the culprits to try to mask where their threats come from.

If the threats keep coming, then one wonders how this will be handled — will this all just continue, or will the threats be ignored at some point?

Dozens of flights across Europe have been impacted by bomb threats this week, which is obviously incredibly frustrating. None were found to be credible, but they’ve all been taken seriously, with everything from aircraft evacuations, to diversions, to fighter jet escorts.

It can be hard to track down the source of these threats, especially if it’s some tech-savvy, foreign operation. An email claiming to be associated with the group behind these threats suggest that this is only the beginning. Only time will tell how this plays out, because if the threats continue and authorities have no way to take action against the foreign culprits, their options might be limited.

What do you make of this Europe airplane bomb threat situation?

Scientists Were Shocked to Discover Yet Another Surprising Benefit of Cranberry Juice

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Cranberry juice is known in certain circles as a helpful way to prevent the risk of future UTIs, but there's more to this beverage than that. Now, new research suggests the drink could also help your teeth...or at least help reduce plaque associated with tooth decay.

The study, which was published in the journal Scientific Reports, found that 100 percent cranberry juice could slow the growth of dental plaque and reduce the production of certain acids that could lead to cavities.

It's important to stress that the findings are preliminary at this point. But dentists say the idea of using cranberry juice to lower the odds of tooth decay isn't as out there as it seems. Here's the deal.

Meet the experts: Mark S. Wolff, DDS, PhD, dean at the University of Pennsylvania School of Dental Medicine; Christine D. Wu, PhD, lead study author and professor in the Department of Pediatric Dentistry at the University of Illinois Chicago; Julie Meyerson, DMD, a dentist in New York City; and Matthew J. Messina, DDS, clinical associate professor at The Ohio State University College of Dentistry

For the study, researchers recruited 22 people (eight adults and 14 kids) and randomly assigned them one of three drinks: unsweetened 100 percent cranberry juice, cranberry juice cocktail (made up of 27 percent juice and added sugar), or water.

The participants didn't brush their teeth the morning of the experiment. To start, researchers collected dental plaque samples that had accumulated overnight with cotton swabs. The participants then rinsed their mouths with water, before having three small amounts of their assigned drink, swishing each around their mouths several times before swallowing.

After 30 minutes, the researchers took plaque samples again. (This time, they took samples from teeth that hadn't been swabbed earlier.) The researchers discovered that people who swished with and drank pure cranberry juice had slower plaque regrowth than those who didn't use the drink.

The pure cranberry juice was also linked with less acid production in dental plaque. (The acids made by oral bacteria can break down tooth enamel and increase the risk of cavities.)

Again, the study was small, so much more research is needed. And though the decision to study cranberry juice for oral health seems random, there's some precedent, according to Christine D. Wu, PhD, lead study author and professor in the Department of Pediatric Dentistry at the University of Illinois Chicago. "[Cranberry juice] has a polyphenol chemical compound called proanthocyanidin," she says. During research on the compound, Wu says her team discovered that proanthocyanidin can impact dental plaque.

"These compounds may interfere with processes involved in plaque formation," says Julie Meyerson, DMD, a dentist in New York City. For example, bacteria like Streptococcus mutans produce enzymes that convert dietary sugars into sticky substances called glucans, which help bacteria attach to teeth and form plaque biofilms, she says. "Cranberry polyphenols may interfere with these processes, making it more difficult for bacteria to adhere and form mature biofilms on teeth," Meyerson says. (That biofilm is also known as plaque.)

Cranberry compounds may also influence the metabolism of bacteria and reduce acid production as a result, she says. "This is important because acids produced by plaque bacteria lower the oral pH, contributing to enamel demineralization and tooth decay," Meyerson says.

Matthew J. Messina, DDS, clinical associate professor at The Ohio State University College of Dentistry, agrees that the proanthocyanidins are likely fueling this link. "These are the same compounds associated with cranberry’s reputation for urinary tract health," he says. "Proanthocyanidins don’t so much kill bacteria as interfere with them."

What we don't know right now is how this will actually impact dental health. "It could very well suppress plaque, but will it affect disease?" says Mark S. Wolff, DDS, PhD, dean at the University of Pennsylvania School of Dental Medicine. "The findings are very preliminary."

Wu says that cranberry juice "may be a functional food" for dental health, but more research is needed.

Messina agrees. If you like cranberry juice, he recommends having unsweetened 100 percent juice over a cranberry juice cocktail. But at the same time, "don’t trade your toothbrush for a juice glass," he says. " Brushing twice a day with fluoride toothpaste, cleaning between teeth, limiting sugar, and seeing your dentist regularly are still what prevent cavities."

Carina Hsieh, MPH, is the deputy features editor of Women’s Health. She has more than a decade’s worth of experience working in media and has covered everything from beauty, fashion, travel, lifestyle, pets, to health.

She began her career as an intern in the fashion closet at Cosmopolitan where she worked her way up to Senior Sex & Relationships Editor. While covering women’s health there, she discovered her passion for health service journalism and took a break to get her Masters in Public Health. Post-grad school, she worked as a freelance writer and as The Daily Beast’s first Beauty, Health, and Wellness Reporter.

Carina is an alum of the Fashion Institute of Technology and the Yale School of Public Health. She and her French Bulldog, Bao Bao, split their time between Brooklyn and Connecticut. She enjoys reformer Pilates, (slow) running, and smelling the fancy toiletries in boutique fitness class locker rooms.

Touch support and Spatial Preview will introduce new ways to create, sculpt and explore 3D work.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The new MacBook Pro PROLED XDR has the best display our design editor has seen on a laptop. It also introduces touch input to an Apple laptop for the first time, and that could change how creatives interact with their software.

Maxon sees a world of potential, announcing that Cinema 4D and ZBrush, long-time features in our guides to best 3D modelling software and the best animation software, will get touch support for the new MacBook in late November. And that's not all. Cinema 4D is also getting a new capability that allows artists to preview 3D creations with Apple Vision Pro.

Maxon believes the new MacBook Pro will introduce an "entirely new dimension to professional creative workflows" with its tandem OLED display, intuitive touch capabilities, and powerful M5 Pro and M5 Max chips. The touch display means artists will have new ways to explore interact directly with their 3D creations.

Whether building and animating complex 3D worlds in Cinema 4D or developing intricate digital sculptures in ZBrush, touch support is intended to bring the responsiveness of hands-on interaction to professional desktop workflows, complementing traditional mouse, keyboard, and trackpad workflows.

Maxon already has ZBrush for iPad, which made digital sculpting in the app more mobile and allowed artists to shape and refine their work with their fingers or Apple Pencil. Touch support on MacBook Pro extends that to the more familiar desktop experience.

While touchscreen displays on Windows laptops have been around for many years, Apple's PROLED XDR Display also delivers exceptional brightness, contrast, and color accuracy.

Meanwhile, upcoming Apple Vision Pro support for Cinema 4D will extend the creative process opening up collaborative, real-time three dimensional workflows. Through the new Spatial Preview API, artists using Mac Virtual Display on Apple Vision Pro will be able to explore, preview, and interact with Cinema 4D designs in immersive life-size detail, making it easier to evaluate proportions and composition before moving forward with production.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

Rather than judging work based on a 2D image, artists and designers will be able to move through an interior, evaluate a proposed installation, or explore the layout of a space at real-world scale before anything is physically built. This could be particularly valuable for architectural visualisation, retail environments, exhibition spaces and experiential design.

The integration also aims to enable more seamless communication with clients and collaborators, allowing them to inhabit the same space at the same time, immersed in the scene or from a bird’s-eye view using Apple Vision Pro. For reviewers who may not be familiar with navigating 3D software, this could allow them to better understand the space, identify potential changes, and provide more informed feedback earlier in the creative process.

Cinema 4D and ZBrush will be immediately available on Apple's new MacBook Pro with PROLED XDR Display, which begins shipping 6 November, 2026. Touch support for Cinema 4D and ZBrush along with Open in Spatial Preview for Apple Vision Pro running visionOS 27 and Mac running macOS 27 will be available in late November.

A new Cinema 4D iPad app is also slated for release before the end of the year. You can learn more on the Maxon website.

The four games they will be delisting on Xbox very soon are as follows:

The delist announcement for all four of these games was shared on Steam, and it only mentions Steam. However, GameMill has done this kind of thing before, and it's usually resulted in delistings for multiple platforms including Xbox.

8 Sculptural Mirrors That Turn Reflection Into Art

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Once a largely practical accessory, mirrors have become a canvas for experimentation with form, material, and light. Contemporary designers are moving beyond conventional frames and flat reflective surfaces, creating sculptural objects that change how we see both ourselves and the spaces around us.

During an opening talk for the inaugural Walker Design Triennial last month, Eleni Petaloti, co-founder of the New York- and Athens-based studio Object of Common Interest, explored how manipulating reflective surfaces can raise questions about memory—another form of reflection. Her observation points to the mirror’s potential beyond its everyday function. Unlike the digital screens that mediate so much of contemporary life, a physical mirror engages directly with its surroundings, responding to light, movement, and the viewer’s presence.

That relationship between object, environment, and viewer runs through the nine designs below. Some treat the frame as the primary sculptural element, while others manipulate the reflective surface itself through color, texture, and unconventional fabrication techniques. From tinted resins and cast bronze to glazed ceramics and hand-worked metals, these mirrors demonstrate how even a familiar household object can become a compelling piece of design.

Collaborating with manufacturer Roll & Hill, Brooklyn-based Bower Studios pushes reflective glass toward sculptural territory with its new Fringe collection. Founders Danny Giannella and Tammer Hijazi explore rich dimensional layering using precise geometric arrangements, integrating warm timber frames that anchor multi-toned mirrored panels interspersed with subtle graphic outlines. This careful composition creates depth across flat planes by interlacing clean silhouettes with tactile nuance.

The Fringe collection is available at rollandhill.com starting at $3,960.

Photography courtesy of MoMA Design Store

Produced for MoMA Design Store by New York-based Ready To Hang, the Jelly Ring Mirror introduces playful, almost childlike sculptural tension to functional home decor. Founder Jeffrey Renz extends his studio’s garment-inspired language by embedding smooth, hand-turned spherical elements around a rounded reflective plane. The resulting silhouette mimics an embrace or compressed form, establishing a subtle dialogue between rigid glass and organic curves. Produced in vibrantly colored glass, this playful wall ornament transforms a straightforward daily ritual into an unexpected visual encounter.

The Jelly Ring Mirror is available at store.moma.org for $995.

Debuted at Salon 94 Design in Manhattan, the Phase collection by Dutch designer Sabine Marcelis examines light dynamics through circular and vertical forms. Utilizing her signature ombré-hued cast resin, Marcelis creates subtly tinted surfaces that filter incoming light alongside reflections of the surrounding environment. The compositions change continuously depending on natural light conditions, reminiscent of quiet lunar cycles and shifting atmospheric moods. Rather than functioning as ordinary looking glasses, these luminous objects blend precise forms with fluid gradient tones, an effect Marcelis has explored in many other applications. The resulting objects invite quiet contemplation.

Inquire about the Phase collection at salon94design.com.

French designer Ronan Bouroullec collaborated with Italian tile producer Mutina to create Carrousel, a series of limited-edition ceramic-framed mirrors offered in various sizes. Covered in high-gloss Gris or Orange glazes, these openwork borders interact dynamically with surrounding sunlight, scattering highlights across domestic rooms as much as the mirrored glass they hold. In his distinctly playful yet restrained style, Bouroullec emphasizes clean, minimalist geometry, showing how traditional ceramic techniques can yield sleek modern accents while retaining the slightly imperfect imprint of the human hand. The reflective glass paired with textured ceramic frames establishes a palpable material presence on the wall.

Carrousel is available at mutina.it. Price available upon request.

Photography courtesy of The Future Perfect

Eindhoven-based Studio RENS introduces Glass Ensemble at New York’s The Future Perfect, a series of mirrors made from fused sheets of upcycled glass. The expansive collection stems from an ongoing exploration of color theory, expressed, in this case, as layered reflective compositions. Designers Renee Mennen and Stefanie van Keijsteren combine the transparent material with colorful foils and polish the resulting surfaces to alter light transmission. Each piece functions as both a three-dimensional geometric accent and a practical mirror, creating subtle tonal shifts that evoke an emotional response.

Glass Ensemble is available at thefutureperfect.com, starting at $4,900.

Photography courtesy of Cristina Grajales Gallery

Presented at Cristina Grajales Gallery in New York as part of Fernando Mastrangelo’s recently debuted Territories collection, The Divide mirror combines raw earthlike textures with refined silvered glass. Mastrangelo casts crushed minerals, colored sand, and cement along the perimeter, creating a stark contrast with the smooth central plane. The subtly variegated gradients appear to erode, suggesting the natural strata of vulnerable coastal landscapes. The viewer sees their own reflection within this imagined yet plausible setting. By treating the frame as the primary expressive element of this functional sculpture, the designer turns a standard looking glass into a work of fine art.

Inquire about The Divide at mastrangelo.co.

Unveiled at New York showroom Otras Formas, the Seasons Mirror by designer Jennifer Choi presents a harmonious study in proportion and craft. Measuring 33 inches square, the wall-mounted piece features a sculpted wooden frame that contours smoothly around its central glass panel. Choi draws inspiration from natural cycles, translating organic shifts into fluid lines and warm tonal variations across carved timber surfaces.

The Seasons Mirror is available at www.otrasformas.co. Price available upon request.

Introduced as part of Blu Dot’s recent releases, the Mojo reimagines classic floor-standing mirrors with a generous organic outline. Standing 79 inches tall, its fluid asymmetrical contours depart from conventional rectangular shapes to reflect the natural form of a roughly hewn rock. The undulating form transforms what’s ordinarily a dressing aid into a dramatic, room-defining centerpiece. The Minneapolis brand pairs accessible manufacturing with impressive proportions.

The Mojo is available at bludot.com for $1,250.

Adrian Madlener is a Brussels-born, New York-based writer specializing in collectible and sustainable design. With a focus on craft-led experimentation, he covers designers and makers who are pushing the boundaries of their disciplines.

AGNT Posts 6% Revenue Growth in Q3, Expects ‘Difficult’ Market Into 2027

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

AGNT, the parent company for eXp Realty and NextHome, posted revenue growth despite economic headwinds of high rates and sluggish home demand, the company announced during an earnings call Thursday.

Closed real estate sales transactions reached 131,000 in the third quarter of 2026, up 8% year-over-year, alongside a 10% jump in sales volume to $59.3 billion, executives said. That productivity pushed AGNT revenues to $1.4 billion over the quarter, up 6% from $1.3 billion a year ago.

Meanwhile, the company saw a net loss of $4.3 million over the quarter and 7% higher operating expenses from a year ago. AGNT’s adjusted EBITDA closed out the quarter at $17.1 million, down 3% from $17.7 million compared to the previous year.

Newly minted AGNT CEO Leo Pareja acknowledged the market is challenging, with existing-home sales expected to be near 4 million, inventory up about 7% year-over-year but still below pandemic levels and mortgage rates above 7.3%. He said the market was “not broken” but “resetting to normal” after two unusually strong booms.

Overall, the company saw agent count grow to 87,457 by the end of the third quarter, up 5% year-over-year (including NextHome, which was acquired earlier in the year).

Pareja attributed most of its agent attrition mostly to unproductive agents leaving eXp or NextHome, with 49% of those nonproductive agents leaving the real estate industry altogether in Q3. Newer agents to the company tend to join a team, where they get a stronger start, he added.

“With 44% of new agent joiners on teams in Q3, and this is important because agents on teams are 77% more productive than individual agents,” Pareja said.

The company’s Q3 earnings call came on the heels of AGNT, along with Keller Williams, HomeServices of America and Real REMAX Group, forming Open Real Estate, a new shared listing-data company. The new initiative uses standard fields for listings to be entered then distributes the data out to participating members, local MLSs, search portals and other platforms.

“When listings are fragmented or kept out of view, buyers can miss homes, and sellers reach fewer potential buyers,” Pareja said. Pareja noted that Open Real Estate’s startup costs have limited exposure for the rest of the business.

As mortgage rates stay north of 7% and home sales slow heading into the final stretch of the year, AGNT updated its guidance for the remainder of 2026. AGNT Chief Financial Officer Jesse Hill acknowledged the tough market conditions the company—and the entire industry—is facing.

“Rising interest rates continue to weigh significantly on housing demand, and we’ve seen that softening as we’ve headed into Q4 here,” Hill said. He added that market conditions, along with AGNT seeing “higher-than-baseline” legal expenses peaking in 2026, caused it to update its financial outlook for the year.

In late October, eXp reached a settlement with agent Anya Roberts who sued former eXp agents Michael Bjorkman and David Golden in 2023, alleging they drugged and sexually assaulted her at company events. Roberts also named the brokerage and founder Glenn Sanford in her federal lawsuit for allegedly ignoring or covering up a sex trafficking/recruiting scheme tied to its revenue-share model. The company has consistently denied all wrongdoing in connection to the lawsuit.

While the case was settled for an undisclosed amount, a separate, larger civil lawsuit from four other female eXp agents with similar allegations is ongoing. Hill said the company expects legal costs to “normalize” in 2027, but remain higher for the rest of the year.

Overall in 2026, AGNT expects revenue to reach $4.85 billion to $5.05 billion and adjusted EBITDA in the neighborhood of $40 million to $47 million, Hill noted.

Deborah Kearns is a freelance editor and writer with more than 15 years of experience covering real estate, mortgages and personal finance topics. Her work has appeared in The New York Times, Forbes Advisor, The Associated Press, MarketWatch, USA Today, MSN and HuffPost, among others. Deborah previously held editorial leadership and writing roles at NerdWallet, Bankrate, LendingTree and RE/MAX World Headquarters.

James Morrison issues firm statement over West Brom star’s Premier League potential

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

West Brom missed the opportunity to move into the Championship’s top two after being held to a 1-1 draw by Southampton on Tuesday night. James Morrison’s side now turn their attention to this weekend’s clash with Millwall, with the Baggies preparing to host the Lions in a bid to strengthen their promotion push.

Ahead of this weekend’s clash, Morrison has been asked about his midfielder Ousmane Diakite, who has been in excellent form this season. The WBA boss was questioned on Diakite’s potential, specifically whether he is a Premier League player in the making, as reported by Steve Tudgay.

Morrison issued a confident response, saying, “Yes he is. With us!” His comments back up those of former WBA man Paul Robinson, who was full of praise for Diakite when speaking to the Express and Star.

“He has Premier League attributes, when you look at him,” the former defender said. “It’s about consistency. We have seen Diakite in the Blackburn game, then the Preston game and now he has gone to that next level against West Ham.

“He stood out against players who are expected to win the league. He destroyed them in the middle of the park.”

Diakite has made an encouraging start to the 2026/27 Championship season, establishing himself as an important figure in the WBA midfield. The 26-year-old has featured in 14 league games, making 11 starts and averaging a FotMob rating of 7.22.

His biggest strengths have been his defensive contributions, recording 25 tackles, 19 interceptions and 58 recoveries. He has also won 81.8% of his aerial duels, placing him in the 95th percentile among midfielders.

Diakite has shown composure in possession too, completing 88.5% of his 544 passes, including 86 line-breaking efforts. His recent performances have been particularly impressive, earning ratings of 7.8 against Southampton and Blackburn and 7.7 against Preston.

Although he is yet to register a goal or assist, his physicality, defensive reliability and ability to retain possession have made him a valuable asset for West Brom.

Morrison is building an exciting, well-balanced midfield at West Brom, combining defensive strength, energy and attacking quality. Diakite and Jayson Molumby provide a solid foundation, while summer signing Felix Horn Myhre adds creativity and goals.

Isaac Price has also emerged as a key attacking threat, already contributing five goals this season. There is promising young talent in Rabby Nzingoula, Toby Collyer and Harry Whitwell, too, giving Morrison options for the future.

The group has helped West Brom make a strong start to the campaign, with its blend of experience and potential offering encouragement for a sustained promotion push.

Farah: Why a Fender Bender in My Porsche Taycan Cost $35,000

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

A minor accident in my late-model EV nearly totaled it, and the battery pack was partly to blame.

I love cars, but I also know cars are bad for the environment, whether they are powered by gas, electric, or diesel. New cars, in particular, take energy and raw materials to build, and often cost more to run and maintain. For me, that upfront environmental impact is all the more reason to keep older, efficient cars on the road even in the face of rising repair costs. But there must be a balance in the quest for efficiency.

If you do care about the planet, the most efficient choice in the U.S. right now is to buy a used, well-maintained EV. No one built a brand new car for you, and the carbon footprint from assembling it is now spread across multiple owners. The longer you drive it, the cleaner it is (versus a combustion car).

For nearly two years, I have been driving around L.A. in what was, as of December of 2024, the cheapest used Porsche Taycan Cross Turismo in the United States. I love EVs for driving around cities just as much as I love a high-compression, light-flywheel, manual gearbox, mid-engined monster for a canyon run: each is fit for its purpose. I did not buy a used EV to make some kind of political or environmental statement—I saw personal value and a fun public experiment. After all, a Taycan depreciates rapidly from new, and not because it’s a badly made car.

What originally stickered for $139,000 was mine for $64,800 plus tax with only 26,000 miles. Over the approximately 12,000 miles since, it has been a superb car: comfortable, fast, dynamic, and overwhelmingly reliable, albeit with a few software hiccups and two genuine mechanical repairs, all sorted under the Porsche Certified pre-owned unlimited-mile warranty. Although a Taycan is still a semi-exotic Porsche to many, the four-seat electric wagon is my family’s most “normal” car.

Which is why I let a friend borrow it for a trip to the lake with his wife. My friend isn’t a car enthusiast, but he had no history of accidents, shenanigans, or other red flags in the 30 years I’ve known him. The perfect person to trust with a loan. And so I never would have predicted the phone call I received over that weekend: He’d been in an accident with my car.

Fortunately, no one was hurt. Also fortunately (for our relationship), the accident wasn’t his fault, as insisted by him, bolstered by a police report, and cosigned by my insurance adjuster. In fact, he was stopped at a red light and rear-end/sideswiped by a young woman who, as it turns out, had a long history of accidents in her short driving career.

And while I was upset to get the phone call, my rage subsided quickly when I saw the accident site photo. This didn’t look like a big deal at all. The rear bumper was scraped, and perhaps the parking sensor was damaged. The painted-over fender needed replacing, as did the HRE 522M wheel. I figured maybe $3000-4000 for the body work, another $4000 for the wheel, a couple of weeks in the shop, and that would be that.

It would be 73 days, and it would cost my insurance company over $35,000 before I got my car back. Hypothetically, if the car was not insured under an agreed-value classic car collector’s policy, it would likely have been outright totaled, despite being easily repairable.

It turns out the impact on the damaged wheel, plus the force of the car being shoved several feet while the brakes were applied, damaged the air suspension. It wasn’t just damaged on the corner that was hit. Both the gas strut and control arm assembly were cracked on the opposite corner as well, even without a direct impact! When the air suspension lost pressure, the air supply unit, control module, and a bunch of other four-figure components went down with it. Once the suspension needs to be disassembled to that degree, Porsche isn’t going to reassemble it with old hardware, so the invoice includes thousands in bolts, bushings, collar nuts, washers, carriers, and so on.

And because it's an EV, performing this work requires removing the high-voltage battery pack: 24 hours at $340 an hour. If you aren’t used to paying for mechanical work, this is a huge rate, even for Porsches, even in Los Angeles.

So while the cosmetic bodywork amounted to little more than $5000 or $6000, the mechanical and electrical repairs escalated like Bitcoin in 2020, eventually landing in the mid-twenties. Factor in a new wheel from HRE, three flatbed transports, and assorted random billables, and that’s how the total made it to $35,000.

The air suspension took second place on the bill; the Achilles' heel for late-model luxury car service, gas or electric alike. It was almost $20,000. This and all other repairs would have been similar to a gas-powered Panamera or Cayenne. But if you're predisposed to hate EVs, here's another piece of ammunition towards that view. Nearly $8000 of this bill, over 20% of it, went to battery pack labor. (The pack itself was, and is, totally fine).

As these once-pricey EVs get cheaper, the cost of repairs raises the value required to avoid getting totaled. This is a shame. Otherwise working, good-condition cars with most of their carbon footprint already amortized might be scrapped. Yes, battery packs and cells can be recycled, but it’s not done at a large enough scale yet. More importantly, the best thing we can do for the environment is keep already-built EV’s on the road for as long as possible. Fortunately, battery packs are lasting longer in cars than originally expected (ironically part of the reason for the slow build of the battery recycling ecosystem).

To their credit, both South Bay Certified Collision Center in Torrance, CA, and McKenna Porsche did an excellent job repairing my car. This is not a story about getting ripped off, and I’m not arguing that any other shop could do it cheaper. The fact that my Taycan got a completely refreshed air suspension means it drives perhaps even better than it did before. And because I have agreed value insurance and the accident wasn’t my fault, none of the enormous stack of dollars it took to fix my car came out of my pocket. I could have chosen to total the car, but I chose to fix it. Such is the benefit of that type of policy. And I intend to keep driving this car because I very much like it.

If we are going to have more EV’s, which we should, because they are good for many things, then we need to ensure that these cars survive minor collisions. That could mean changing what the word “totaled” means for an EV on the insurance side. It could also mean training up enough technicians so that a battery pack removal doesn’t command a higher hourly rate than what I pay for Lamborghini Countach restoration. It should also mean that car companies, perhaps German ones, should design battery packs so that they’re more easily removed or that other major components can be replaced around them.

In either case, we can’t have EVs meeting a premature demise. There’s nothing more wasteful than a car that could be working, but without working math to justify fixing it.

Matt Farah is a lifelong car enthusiast who began his automotive career at dealerships, rental agencies, and detail shops before discovering the power of YouTube in 2006, with his channel The Smoking Tire. Farah has a Bachelors of Fine Arts from the University of Pennsylvania, with a concentration in Photography, helping not only create YouTube content but also providing his own photography for his Editor-at-Large position at Road & Track.

He has hosted and produced television series on NBC Sports, G4 Network, SPEED, and Esquire. The Smoking Tire Podcast is #1 in the category every week of the year. Now at 800+ episodes, The Smoking Tire podcast is the definitive guest stop for who’s who in the auto industry. Farah’s Westside Collector Car Storage is a game-changer in luxury, concierge parking that expanded to a second location in 2023.

Janji’s New Winter Collection Has All the Gear You Need for Cold-Weather Running

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The lineup includes jackets, vests, fleece tights, and more.

We earn a commission for products purchased through some links in this article. Why Trust Us?

Two types of winter runners exist: those who brave the rain, snow, and cold, and those who head for the treadmill until spring. If you fall into that former camp, now’s the time to stock up on crucial winter running gear, and Janji’s newly released Winter 2026 collection has just about everything you need for cold-weather running.

This is a hefty drop, including men’s, women’s, and unisex gear to cover you from head to toe. Here are some highlights, and you can shop the full collection at Janji.

One of our favorite layers for wintertime running, the Thermalrunner offers a nearly perfect combo of warmth and breathability. It's made with a durable, wind-resistant nylon shell, and its PrimaLoft Gold Active synthetic insulation sits in strategic panels to keep you warm in key areas while letting you dump heat as you move. It also includes three zippered pockets and packs down into the chest pocket for easy storage.

Designed for low weight, breathability, and warmth, this streamlined hoodie is a great pick for blustery weather. The recycled polyester mesh fabric has open pores to vent sweat vapor and excess heat, and it also dries quickly, so sweat won’t leave you soaked and freezing. Inside, the fabric is brushed for a cozy feel that takes the edge off cold temperatures. It’s available as a men’s jacket and as a women’s vest, too.

When winter brings its worst, reach for these pants. A totally new addition to Janji’s lineup, the Thermalrunner Hybrid Insulated Jogger packs serious heat with its panels of synthetic insulation across the front and seat and an interior fleece lining. On the outside, the smooth nylon face fabric resists moisture and doesn’t “swish,” according to Janji, and an external drawcord waist ensures a slip-free fit, even when you load up the pants’ three zippered pockets.

The Winter Collection includes several pairs of gloves and a cozy hat, but I think this balaclava wins for its versatility. You can wear it as a neck gaiter, as a hat, or as a full balaclava-style head covering to power through arctic conditions. No matter how you wear it, its nylon-merino-elastane blend delivers breathable warmth and a snug, comfortable fit.

Compass ‘Welcomes’ Open Real Estate, Sees Record Quarterly Earnings

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

As Open Real Estate makes headlines around the future of listing flow and control, Compass CEO Robert Reffkin addressed the initiative in the company’s third quarter earnings call, saying Compass “welcome(s) this change.”

Reffkin said that Open Real Estate’s plan “actually validates” Compass’s messaging over recent years, and he is “excited to see the four of them lead in this way and just see others join.”

In terms of whether or not Compass itself would join the initiative, Reffkin said “there’s definitely a path.”

“Compass International Holdings is definitely excited that there will be a national real estate listing service that will return the power back to the creator of the listing, the homeowner and their trusted agent, and that is good news for all agents and for all their clients, and we’re looking forward to seeing if there’s a way for us to be involved,” he continued.

Compass reported a “record” quarter, beating out expectations it set for itself in Q2. Reffkin touted the firm’s success amid an array of market challenges, from increasing mortgage rates to decreasing home sales.

Compass’s adjusted EBITDA (earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization) of $306 million for Q3 surpassed the company’s original expectations of $275 million to $305. While down from $363 million in Q2, EBITDA rose from $94 million in Q3 2025 to set a new Q3 record.

Other Q3 records for Compass include revenue, which came in at $3.9 billion for Q3. While this marks a dip from last quarter’s $4.31 billion, it represents a whopping 111% year-over-year increase from $1.85 billion in Q3 2025. Pro forma revenue was up 11.6% year-over-year from $3.49 billion.

Net income was also at a record Q3 level, coming in at $62 million. While down from $92 million in Q2, the increase is significant compared to the $4 million net loss year over year. The company has now been net income positive for three consecutive quarters.

Much of the quarter’s success was attributed to improvements in Compass’s operating expenses (OPEX), which were down 6% year-over-year. Free cash flow was also improved at $240 million in Q3, which the company noted was a record for any quarter or year in its history.

CFO Scott Wahlers said that bettering the company’s OPEX came from “just good old-fashioned tightening our belt” in a market that is experiencing challenges from “worse than normal seasonality.”

Looking at the brokerage business, gross transaction volume for Coldwell Banker, Compass, Corcoran and Sotheby’s International Realty came in at $138.9 billion, down from $155.2 billion in Q2 but up 96.5% year-over-year from $70.7 billion in Q3 2025. Pro forma, transaction volume was up 7.5% year-over-year to $133.1 billion.

Brokerage transactions were up 106.4% year-over-year, from 67,887 in Q3 2025 to  140,118 this quarter. Pro forma, transactions were actually down 0.3% year-over-year, but this is compared to a 1.8% decrease across the entirety of the U.S. residential real estate market (as sourced from NAR’s existing home sales data).

For franchise business, gross transaction volume was at $108.9 billion, a 1,181% year-over-year increase from $8.5 billion in Q3 2025. Pro forma, transaction volume was up 2.3% year-over-year to $104.2 billion. Franchise transactions were up a whopping 2,073% year-over-year, from 8,825 in Q3 2025 to 191,809 this quarter. Pro forma, transactions actually decreased 3.6% year-over-year.

Reffkin also called out some business that will be coming in from Wall Street due to bonuses from working with and investing in AI.

“(Wall Street) will have record bonuses coming in the months ahead, and that will lead to more transactions,” he said. “There are a number of partners on Wall Street who have asked me for an introduction to our agents, and I have made those introductions.”

The mortgage business improved as well, credited to Compass’s acquisitions. Total mortgage transactions were up 406% year-over-year to 38,757 (up from 7,666 in Q3 2025). This was also up pro forma by 1.7% year-over-year. Loan volume was $3.30 billion, up 9.9% year-over-year on a pro forma basis.

Compass just recently inked a deal with fintech company Rate to unite the two companies’ mortgage ventures—Guaranteed Rate Affinity and OriginPoint—under the OriginPoint brand. The merger shifts OriginPoint up to a top 10 mortgage lender, which Reffin said is “incredible after such a short amount of time.”

“Longer term, we want to be a top five retail lender,” he added.

Agent retention and recruiting also improved for Compass due to what the company and Reffkin outlined as a strong value prop and industry positioning. For agents producing more than $20,000 in gross commission income, retention is above 98%.

Recruiting-wise, brokerage agents specifically increased by 2,292 in Q3, and total agent count grew by 6,390.

Reffkin also attributed Compass’ Q3 success to the rollout of its “Home” platform, an end-to-end proprietary technology for real estate professionals.

Across Coldwell Banker, Corcoran and Sotheby’s International Realty, Compass stated it saw 33,950 agents adopt the available platform, bringing the total number of users across the full company to 68,349 agents. The plan remains to roll out the platform to the full portfolio of Compass brands in Q1 2027.

Through the platform, all participating agents will have access to the company’s Private Exclusive and Coming Soon inventory.

In talking about Compass’s platform, Reffkin dove into the company’s “See It First” campaign, calling it “part of a rebranding exercise.”

“I think some of our competitors have tried to brand the listings that we have as hidden, clearly,” he explained. “All of our listings are available to all agents and to all buyers, and when you call them See It First, then it’s pretty clear we just want you to see it first on our site, which is a natural thing for a company to want to be able to do.”

Reffkin reported that the company will take a conservative approach in the months ahead as the market continues to be challenged.

“There are reasons to be optimistic, but obviously we’re not planning for that,” he said. “We continue to focus on costs and plan for being conservative. But I think what can help in our favor (is) one, we have more inventory right now than any point since…2019. ..More inventory means you can create more sales.”

When asked about future merger-and-acquisition opportunities, Reffkin indicated the door is open.

“We continue to be a company that wants to support great operators and continue to have those conversations.”

Compass stated it expects a Q4 revenue of $3.150 billion to $3.350 billion, and a Q4 adjusted EBITDA of $100 million to $130 million. For the full year of 2026, the company stated it expects free cash flow to be positive, and OPEX of $2.71 billion to $2.73 billion.

Maria Patterson contributed to this report.

Claudia Larsen is a senior editor for RISMedia.

Jorge Jesus breaks silence on Cristiano Ronaldo's Portugal retirement

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Jorge Jesus insists he has no regrets over the rift with Ronaldo, who brought an end to his 23-year Portugal career without informing the national team manager

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Jorge Jesus has insisted he bears no responsibility for Cristiano Ronaldo’s decision to step away from international football.

The 41-year-old forward called time on his Portugal career on Thursday, finishing with men's record totals of 234 caps and 146 goals.

His announcement arrived under a month after receiving a backdated 30-day ban and a 255 euro (roughly £210) fine from the Portuguese Football Federation. The sanction was imposed after the forward walked out on the squad before a Nations League clash with Denmark on 1 October, following a disagreement with Jesus.

With the suspension served, Ronaldo was eligible for selection for upcoming fixtures against Denmark and Wales.

Unveiling his squad for those games on Friday, Jesus – who managed Ronaldo at Saudi club Al Nassr prior to taking the Portugal job in July – maintained he felt no guilt over the end of the five-time Ballon d’Or winner’s 23-year career on the international stage.

Jesus said: "No, maybe I rushed things, but not because of me. It was in his head, more call-ups, fewer call-ups.

"We also talked about it a few times. And if it was going to happen. But no, I don’t feel guilty about anything regarding his departure. I respect his decision.

"And, as such, his story cannot be ignored. He will always be a symbol and I have built a strong friendship with him over this year and a half.

"Sporting-wise, he made a decision and I greatly respect his decision."

The 72-year-old manager explained that he received no advance notice of Ronaldo’s retirement prior to its publication on social media, confirming he would have otherwise been named in the squad.

"I found out when we all did," Jesus said. "Until then, I knew nothing about it.

"He was playing, he was scoring goals; he was one of the players on my squad list."

Ronaldo had alleged that Jesus had "broken his word" by keeping him on the bench throughout a match against Norway on 27 September. The forward spent time warming up during the second period in Oslo before remaining an unused substitute, subsequently departing the team camp.

Responding to questions regarding the rift, Jesus said: "All my decisions were made with the team in mind.

"As a manager, I have always defended what was best for the team. I am at peace with my decision."

Jesus noted that he had not spoken with Ronaldo following Thursday’s statement.

Offering a tribute to the departing forward, PFF president Pedro Proenca said: "No tribute will ever be enough to thank him for everything he has done for Portuguese football.

"Thank you for everything, captain. This is and always will be your home."

Bringing Bloodborne back is tricky because "there's a lot of involved rights associated," according to former PlayStation boss

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Every year, Bloodborne fans pray for the birth of another game set in FromSoftware's Lovecraftian world. Every year, we're no closer to a Bloodborne 2 or even a Bloodborne Remastered. But why? PlayStation's former head honcho, Shawn Layden, blames it on the tricky situation of rights ownership.

Asked about the noticeable lack of Bloodborne in the decade since its blood moon shone down on us all, in an interview with The Trophy Room podcast, Layden explained that returning to any beloved game is tough.

"Bringing back old games has a certain challenge to it," he said. "Can you do something that is not only respectful of the original, but can you, you know, recreate the phenomenon as it were?"

Bloodborne's situation is slightly more complicated, though. It seems that the rights are somehow split between original publisher PlayStation, developer FromSoftware and the studio's primary stakeholder Kadokawa Corporation.

"There's a lot of involved rights associated with it, and in something like that - where Sony's in there and [FromSoftware] is in there and From's parent company is in there - I think just getting everyone to sign off on the same thing is probably the biggest challenge there," Layden said.

Having led the PlayStation brand during most of its PS4 golden years, Layden said working with FromSoftware on Bloodborne and the original Demon's Souls was "fantastic."

Despite endless calls for a comeback and very stable sales more than a decade on, Bloodborne itself is still stuck on the PS4 - though it's still playable via backward compatibility on the PS5, I should add. The closest we seemingly got to a Bloodborne comeback came from Bluepoint Games, which reportedly pitched a remake sometime before parent company PlayStation shut down the acclaimed studio, though FromSoftware supposedly didn't want the project to go ahead.

Another former PlayStation executive, Shuhei Yoshida, previously speculated that Bloodborne hasn't been remade because game director and soulslike pioneer, Hidetaka Miyazaki, would prefer to handle it himself: "Miyazaki-san really, really loved Bloodborne, what he created… So I think he is interested but he's so successful and he's so busy, so he cannot do it himself, and he doesn't want anyone else to touch it."

Elsewhere, a Bloodborne animated feature is in the works with YouTuber JackSepticEye attached as a co-producer.

Tiffani Amber Thiessen Talks Parenting Teens, Period Talks & Her Witchy Era

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The “Saved By The Bell” alum has come full circle and is now playing a principal and mom.

Tiffani Amber Thiessen’s face is burned into the brains of every elder Millennial who watched countless Saved by the Bell episodes and dreamed of dressing exactly like Kelly Kapowski. Of course, we’ve all left the 1900s behind and have grown into adults — parents and women with careers.

Like the rest of us, the 52-year-old mom of two kids — Harper, 16, and Holt, 11 — has left high school in the rear view mirror, but her career continues to flourish. And she’s still in the classroom, in a way: Coven Academy, the witchy dark academia series in which she stars as the principal of Crescent Veil Academy, just got renewed for a second season.

You can also find her on Instagram, where her account is a delightful mix of food, fashion, humor, nostalgia, and life as a woman who is finding power and peace as she enters her 50s.

Recently, Thiessen partnered with Always on its “Period Talks Don’t Have to Be Scary” campaign, which addresses what is often a real hurdle for parents: talking about periods with their kids. Research has found that 43% of moms struggle to start the first-period talk because they feel unprepared. So Thiessen wants to share her own experiences while offering practical advice to parents who want to open and informative with their tweens and teens... even if can be awkward at times.

She recently sat down with Scary Mommy to talk about periods, parenting, her new show, and her family’s holiday traditions.

Scary Mommy: Gen Z and Millennial moms have done a lot to break period stigma with our kids. How have you done that with your kids?

Tiffani Amber Thiessen: I think the generation I grew up in didn’t talk about these things. Now kids are much more open, and I have a very open relationship with both my children. It never fully takes away the scariness of intimate talks, whether it’s periods, the body, or growing up. But I think parents who understand that it isn’t one big, intimidating conversation, but many small, casual talks, can take the edge off.

Partnering with Always has been lovely because they’re trying to prepare parents with what to do and how to have that first conversation. Being prepared gives you the confidence.

SM: I found that I was a lot more scared than my daughter was. Did you feel that?

TAT: Most parents feel that way. You don’t realize until afterward that it really isn’t that scary. I found it to be a bonding moment. I’d tell my kids that over half the population menstruates, so it’s not a big deal. Keeping it casual and a little funny helps. Building that competence between parent and child is what lets your daughter take it on, and it’s a badge of honor.

SM: I love asking this: What’s your best piece of parenting advice?

TAT: It goes back to having an open conversation about anything. When I became a parent, I wanted to be open to receiving anything my kids share, whether it’s hard or difficult. I wanted them to feel comfortable and know I would never judge. We’ve all been in crazy places, and my kids are super open with me, about the fun stuff and the stuff that isn’t so fun. Keeping that conversation free of judgment is probably the most important thing in parenting.

SM: Have you ever wanted to judge, though?

TAT: I can secretly judge, is that okay? [laughs]

TAT: Most of the things my kids tell me I’ve either witnessed, been through, or know someone who has. It’s really not that crazy.

SM: Let’s switch gears to Coven Academy. And you just got renewed for Season 2?

TAT: Monday! We had high hopes and wanted to find an audience that saw the specialness of the show, and I think we did. It’s done very well on Disney+ and Hulu, so we’re over the moon to do it all over again.

SM: There are so few shows made for this demographic: tweens, mostly girls. It’s so good to have something that speaks to kids in that space.

TAT: The witch aspect is having a moment on social media, but it’s not really about spells and potions. It’s about found family, sisterhood, and having each other’s backs. I have a teenage daughter, and she has a wonderful group of girls she can count on. Everybody should have that, especially women.

SM: How has it been going from being a student on a hugely popular show to a headmistress on set with the younger generation?

TAT: It feels natural. I have kids of my own now. The last few shows I’ve been fortunate to do have had younger casts, and I really enjoy working with young adults. I may give them advice, but I get a lot from them, too. We did a lot of night shoots, and I’d see these kids at 3 a.m. doing their TikToks while I was struggling. Their energy kept me going through those nights.

SM: The holidays are coming up. Do you have any family traditions you want to share?

TAT: We’re very traditional in that it’s all about family. We do Christmas morning and Christmas Eve dinner. For the last 15 or 16 years, we’ve alternated between my husband’s family in Texas and ours. This year it’s our turn to go to Texas. We leave the day after Christmas and spend the week and New Year’s with my in-laws. All the cousins are together, and we look forward to it every year.

SM: What have you been cooking lately, or what are you planning for the holidays?

TAT: My kids like the traditions. Thanksgiving is classic turkey and stuffing, plus my mom’s famous cream cheese pie. For Christmas we usually do enchiladas, which I’ll probably make again this year. We usually switch it up for Christmas because everybody’s over turkey.

SM: Dennis Haskins recently passed away. He was your principal on Saved by the Bell, and now you’re playing a principal. Do you have any remembrances to share?

TAT: I have so many. He was our solid one; our anchor. I know it was probably hard for him, being the only adult on a kids’ show. He handled it with such grace and gave us a safe place. He was part of us, and he could be young, but he was still a strong personality. He always made us feel protected, and he’s very much missed.

This interview has been edited and condensed for clarity.

He is also the author of the Health Care Inc. newsletter. You can reach Bob on Signal at bobjherman.09.

The political environment is about to get friendlier for hospitals.

With Democrats gaining control of the House and Senate, hospitals will not have to worry about Republican-led efforts to cut Medicaid payments further. In fact, Democrats have explicit goals to prioritize measures that are financially beneficial to the hospital industry.

Within the first 100 hours of the next Congress, which starts in January, Democrats want to advance policies that would reverse Medicaid cuts outlined in President Trump’s tax law and “permanently restore” the enhanced subsidies for Affordable Care Act plans that expired earlier this year, according to a memo from party leaders. Democrats even plan to block a niche drug discount model, despised by hospitals, that the Trump administration proposed.

Same Price, Different Stellar: 69% Growth Outruns XLM On a Red-Day

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Home » Altcoins » Same Price, Different Stellar: 69% Growth Outruns XLM On a Red-Day

Stellar continues to add regulated issuers, infrastructure partners, and measurable on-chain volume.

Stellar keeps stacking real-world asset activity and on-chain usage even as its token trades near multi-month lows.

On a day when the broader crypto market shed roughly $120 billion and XLM dropped about 9%, the network still ranked first among tracked chains in tokenized fund growth, adding $291 million.

The $291 million single-day print was not an isolated event.

Over the past ten days the network has added several institutional markers: Janus Henderson launched the first Nasdaq-licensed tokenized fund on Stellar, DTCC went live with Stellar as the first public chain in its setup.

Stellar DEX volume jumped 69% in a week, and almost all of it came from a single protocol.Aquarius, the liquidity protocol on @StellarOrg ($XLM), drove nearly the entire increase, per DeFiLlama.The data ⬇️The full story: https://t.co/ZuB0XcyCRC pic.twitter.com/AZKWxDxcrm

MetaMask began giving every account a Stellar address, Securitize signaled plans to expand, Bell went live on testnet, and Franklin Templeton’s presence on the network reached $1.1 billion.

These moves sit on top of longer-term figures. Stellar has repeatedly led or ranked near the top in tokenized fund inflows across 24-hour, 90-day, and year-to-date windows, even while XLM itself has lagged the network’s asset growth.

Separately, Stellar’s decentralized exchange volume rose 69% over the past week. Nearly the entire increase came from Aquarius, the network’s leading liquidity management protocol, according to DeFiLlama data.

Aquarius has been the dominant DEX by both volume and transactions for some time. Its 30-day volume recently sat near $95 million with cumulative volume above $800 million, and it has held the top spot by total value locked among Stellar DEXs.

🚨 BOOM: Crypto lost $120 billion yesterday. $XLM fell 9%.Same day, #Stellar ranked #1 of all tracked chains in tokenized fund growth: +$291 million.The last 10 days on Stellar:Janus Henderson launched the first Nasdaq-licensed tokenized fund.DTCC went live, with Stellar… pic.twitter.com/zgT0u6tW4C

The rest of the chain’s DeFi activity remains concentrated as well: a handful of protocols account for most of the locked value and trading.

The volume jump is real, yet total value locked on Aquarius has stayed relatively flat even during stronger trading months. That pattern suggests more activity is flowing through existing pools rather than a broad influx of new capital.

XLM is trading back near levels seen before some of the recent institutional announcements.

The gap between network metrics and token price has drawn direct questions from observers: who is required to buy more XLM as tokenized assets and DEX volume grow, and what mechanism turns that activity into sustained demand for the native token?

So far, Stellar’s progress in regulated funds and concentrated DeFi has not produced a matching lift in XLM.

Institutional products on the network often settle in other assets, and much of the on-chain trading runs through a small set of protocols whose fees and incentives do not automatically translate into broad XLM buying.

The ex-England goalkeeper said that she and her partner had been ‘keeping a little secret’ from fans in announcing the news

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Former England goalkeeper Mary Earps has revealed the birth of a “secret” baby boy with partner Kitty Forrest, saying she was “over the moon” to share the news.

The ex-Lioness, who revealed her relationship with Forrest a year ago, had not previously shared that the pair were expecting a child.

Earps revealed that ‘Baby Forrest Earps’, whose first name she did not disclose, was born on 5 November, announcing the news in an Instagram post chronicling the couple’s pregnancy and a YouTube video.

She said at the beginning of the video: “I’ve been keeping a little secret from you all, and I don’t think I can talk my way out of this one, so I’m going to have to show you.”

On Instagram she wrote: “I can’t quite believe I’m writing this, but yesterday Kitty and I welcomed our beautiful baby boy into the world!

“We’re absolutely over the moon and still trying to take it all in. It’s been such an emotional journey getting to this point, and we couldn’t be more excited to begin this next chapter of our lives as a family of three.

“Already a full-time footballer, and now a full-time mum too! There’s so much I’m looking forward to sharing with you all as I navigate life in football, motherhood and everything in between. I can’t wait to bring you along for the ride!

“For now, we’re soaking up every second of our little bubble. Thank you for all the love and support along the way. It means more than you know, and I’m so excited to share this new adventure with you all.

“Baby name to be revealed! Our greatest adventure starts here.”

Earps was an integral part of the title-winning Lionesses squad at Euro 2022 and reached the World Cup final with England the following year. She retired from international football last summer.

A former Manchester United shot-stopper, Earps spent the last two seasons at Paris Saint-Germain before signing for London City Lionesses this summer.

Former England teammates including Alex Greenwood, Lucy Bronze, Chloe Kelly and Ella Toone were among those to offer their congratulations to the couple.

Romano: Milan remain obsessed with Inacio but Fonseca’s arrival could change things

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

It’s no secret that AC Milan will sign a defender in January, and one name is sitting firmly in the lead but he’s not alone.

Since the summer, Milan have been obsessed with the idea of signing Goncalo Inacio. His name was constant through the mercato and on deadline day, the Rossoneri tried to pounce on a last-minute deal. However, it was unsuccessful, understandably.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Within days of the mercato shutting, the Rossoneri were linked with another move in the January window and these suggestions have not gone away in the months since. Other names have been suggested, but again, Inacio remains the priority.

Speaking on his YouTube channel today, Fabrizio Romano offered an update on the club’s pursuit for Inacio ahead of the January mercato, via MilanNews.

“A defender will definitely arrive. He was supposed to arrive in the summer; he will arrive in January, so it’s no mystery. Milan also persistently sought him during the summer transfer window, an extra defender obviously in addition to Mario Gila, who was also an excellent investment by Milan, so the idea is to bring in at least another one.

“Milan reinstated Tomori a minute after the end of the summer transfer window out of necessity, but Milan’s idea is to take an extra defender. So a defender will definitely arrive.

“The name of Gonçalo Inácio must always be kept there because it is a name that has been in Milan’s mind since the first day of the summer transfer window; from when Ruben Amorim arrived until the last minute of the transfer window, he has always been in the thoughts anyway. There have been different attempts with different formulas.

“Milan had even considered, at a certain point at the end of August, a possible very expensive loan with an option to buy, so several solutions were considered that Sporting did not accept.

“Among other things, Sporting has changed its skin; Paolo Fonseca arrived on the bench, so we’ll see how much this will impact the dynamics of this operation. Clearly he’s not the only name, but Inacio is certainly a very welcome player; Milan has excellent relations with his agents, who are the same as Amorim.

“But I repeat, it doesn’t end here; there’s a list and Milan will make their choices.”

It’s a positive start that a defender will ‘definitely’ arrive; it’s just a case of whether they’re the right man now with Inacio not seeming like the definitive candidate.

Apollo-backed Brightspeed seeks up to $3B from creditors for fibre

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The rural broadband provider has about $10B of debt and has warned it may not survive as a going concern.

Fibre optic cables connected to telecommunications equipment in a network rack.

Brightspeed, a US fibre broadband provider owned by Apollo, is in talks with creditors to raise $2B to $3B of new money, Bloomberg reports. It has about $10B of debt, has warned about its survival, and may file for bankruptcy as soon as December.

Brightspeed, an Apollo-backed US fibre broadband provider, is in talks to raise $2B to $3B of new money from its creditors. That is according to Bloomberg, which cited people familiar with the matter on Friday. The amount, and whether the deal will go through a bankruptcy court, are still being worked out.

Brightspeed and Apollo declined to comment. The Wall Street Journal reported last week that the company could file for bankruptcy as soon as December, Light Reading reported.

Brightspeed has about $10B of debt and warned in September that it may not be able to continue as a going concern. Nearly all its debt trades at distressed levels: one $2.5B loan was quoted at about 17 cents on the dollar on Friday.

Brightspeed was formed in 2022, when Apollo bought Lumen’s local phone and broadband business in 20 states for $7.5B. It is spending heavily to bring fibre to mostly rural and suburban areas there. It passed 3 million fibre-ready locations in April, and fibre sales are up 16% from a year earlier.

It has been through this before. In 2024, Brightspeed cut $1.1B of debt and raised $3.7B of new capital in a deal with its lenders. This month, it named Shawn O’Donnell, a former senior managing director at FTI Consulting, as chief executive.

Apollo is better known in tech circles for its lending to AI, including a $36B debt deal it is arranging with Blackstone to buy chips for Anthropic. Lumen, meanwhile, has turned to AI customers, and this week agreed to sell AI networks with Microsoft.

UPay to Add On-chain Risk Screening with WIDTH’s DART

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

UPay will add on-chain risk screening through a partnership with WIDTH, a compliance and risk technology provider. Under the agreement, announced on 6 November, the digital asset payment platform will use WIDTH’s DART to screen wallet addresses and transactions and give reviewers more detail on what triggered a risk flag.

Digital asset payments are bringing on-chain funds into everyday spending, which can make risk reviews more complex. Reviewers may need to check who the customer is, which wallet addresses were involved and how the funds moved.

DART’s wallet address risk screening (KYA) can identify links between an address and high-risk entities. Its transaction risk screening (KYT) examines where funds came from and where they went. This allows reviewers to trace each result back to the screening criteria and transaction records behind it.

“Our focus is on making risk findings useful to the people reviewing them,” said Chionh Chye Kit, Co-Founder and CEO of WIDTH. “Scores, matched rules and transaction paths help explain why an address or transaction needs attention.”

UPay will use the screening results to support manual reviews and follow-up under its own risk policies.

“Our aim is to give reviewers more than a risk flag,” said Owen Yang, CEO of UPay. “We want them to understand what triggered it and see the transaction history behind it, so they can decide what needs further investigation.”

Screening requirements can vary by product, how funds move and where a service is offered. UPay and WIDTH will agree which payment processes DART will cover and how the findings will feed into reviews. The scope will depend on supported assets, blockchain networks and business arrangements, with both companies evaluating DART’s use based on feedback.

Following the partnership, DART’s findings will supplement UPay’s own customer due diligence and independent review. However, UPay remains responsible for its own business decisions and for meeting applicable regulatory obligations.

Founded in 2023, UPay is a financial technology company that offers digital wallets, payment cards, OTC services, crypto payments, cross-border settlement, remittance and enterprise APIs.

Progress Residential to pay $49.8M to settle FTC, state claims over rental practices

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Progress Residential, the single-family rental operator owned by Pretium Partners, agreed to a $49.8 million settlement with the Federal Trade Commission and state regulators over allegations it hid rental fees, mishandled security deposits and misled tenants about property conditions.   AI Summary

Progress Residential, the property manager of single-family rental homes owned by Pretium Partners, has agreed to pay $49.8 million to settle deceptive and unfair practices claims by the Federal Trade Commission (FTC) and state regulators. The company denies any wrongdoing.

In a complaint filed in the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of Georgia, regulators alleged that the company advertised rental prices that did not reflect the full amount tenants would be required to pay, imposed hidden or inadequately disclosed fees and charges, and mishandled security deposits.

The claims also included that Progress misrepresented the condition and move-in readiness of properties, restricted tenants’ ability to share reviews, and failed to adequately address tenant complaints and maintenance issues.

In a statement given to HousingWire, a representative of Progress Residential and Pretium said the companies have reached an agreement “with the FTC and four states to resolve the Commission’s Civil Investigative Demand related to certain aspects of our resident experience during the period of 2021 to 2025.”

“The agreement contains no admission of wrongdoing, and we maintain Progress’ practices have been transparent, fair, and consistent with industry practice,” the spokesperson said. “Although we disagree with the FTC’s claims, this resolution allows us to move forward with our mission of expanding access to quality, affordable rental housing in neighborhoods of opportunity.”

The deal was reached by the FTC, the attorneys general of Arizona, Indiana and Colorado, and the Utah Division of Consumer Protection. It combines monetary relief for renters across the country.

“We will always welcome investment in housing and recognize the important role private capital can play in increasing opportunities for Hoosier families,” Indiana Attorney General Todd Rokita said in a statement. “But no company, regardless of size or financial backing, gets a free pass to mislead consumers, impose hidden charges or treat tenants unfairly.”

“Consumers searching for housing should have access to straightforward information about the costs associated with renting a home,” said Christopher Mufarrige, director of the FTC’s Bureau of Consumer Protection. “Transparency regarding rental prices and fees helps consumers compare options and make informed choices. Clear pricing practices also support competition by allowing housing providers to compete on the basis of price, quality and service.

“The FTC is monitoring the rental housing market and will continue to take action where appropriate to ensure that consumers receive clear and accurate information about the costs of renting a home.”

Per the settlement terms, Progress must disclose the total monthly rental price in advertisements and listings, along with mandatory fees and charges, before a consumer commits to rent. The company also must stop withholding funds for ordinary wear and tear and is prohibited from misleading claims about property conditions.

The consumer redress funds created by the settlement total $48.5 million and will be used for relief as directed by the stipulated order entered in the federal case. Arizona is receiving $1.25 million.

In 2024, Invitation Homes, the largest owner of U.S. single-family rental homes, agreed to pay $48 million to consumers and change some of its practices.

Editor’s note: This story was updated with a statement from the FTC.

Tesla & SpaceX Lost $420 Billion In Market Cap This Week — Didn’t Have To Be This Way

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Many of you may remember that Tesla and SpaceX got significant boosts early on in their corporate lives from the Obama administration, the Democrat-controlled House, and the Democrat-controlled Senate. There’s a solid chance these companies would no longer exist today without that early support. Democrats in California and in the US government were also critical to consumer support for electric vehicles, air pollution standards for electric vehicles, and carbon credit markets that have led to billions of dollars of revenue for Tesla. Tesla would most certainly not have been able to rise and evolve as it did without all of that support — and, again, it’s questionable whether the company would have ever gotten profitable and been alive today if it didn’t have that strong support from Democrats. Republicans in power have not been willing to support EVs, put stronger pollution limits on gasmobiles, or require higher levels of fuel economy from automakers. Every time they have all the power of the US government, they go the other direction on these things.

Of course, we know that Elon Musk shifted to the right politically (to the far, far right) after a few things happened. Those key things were as follows:

Overall, historically, Democrats have been the ones who have pushed strong policies in support of the clean technologies Tesla develops and sells. However, because of the above (as well as a ton of seeming corruption and anti-democratic, oligarchic influence), Musk has been one of the top funders and campaigners for the Republican Party in recent elections. In this most recent midterm election, he was the single largest individual donor in the country, providing at least $447 million to try to get Republicans elected — completely unsuccessfully (well, like 99.99% unsuccessfully or something like that).

If Elon Musk had continued on his previous rather nonpartisan or bipartisan tendencies, who knows what this election would have meant for Tesla (and SpaceX). However, now that he has become the #1 Republican donor in the United States, and heavily stacked his social media platform in support of Republicans, one has to wonder how Democrats in control of Congress — and perhaps later on the presidency — will respond to the countless attacks Musk has waged against them. It’s logical to assume there will be repercussions. Perhaps they won’t target him unfairly, but they may well choose to investigate and seek punishments for illegal activity, provable corruption, and undemocratic behavior. Instead of this gigantic blue wave being something we could expect to help Tesla, everyone is now wondering if Tesla is going to face some serious consequences.

As a result, reportedly, Tesla and SpaceX lost $420 billion (yes, billion) in market cap following the election — ironically. Perhaps that value has changed now, but how fitting is it that this is the punishment the market is delivering for Elon Musk being so massively wrong politically. For now.

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →
